Remark 2.5
.

Note that
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})
is a subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})
, so we can also define an induction
\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})}_{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})}(V):=\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})}V
. The two induction functors are naturally isomorphic given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{ind}^{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})}_{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})}_{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})}(V)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{a\in\Gamma}a\otimes f(a)

For
(\sigma,W)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G})
, we define
(\gamma^{*}{\sigma})(g):=\sigma(\gamma(g))

From
[
16
]
, for an irreducible representation
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})
either

\bullet
If
\mathrm{Res}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\pi)
is irreducible, i.e.
\mathrm{Res}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\pi)=\sigma
, then
\sigma\simeq\gamma^{*}\sigma
and there are two extensions of
\sigma
to
\mathcal{G}^{+}
.

or

\bullet
If
\mathrm{Res}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\pi)
is reducible, i.e.
\mathrm{Res}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\pi)=\sigma_{1}\oplus\sigma_{2}
, then
\sigma_{2}\simeq{\gamma^{*}\sigma_{1}}
and there is only one extension of
\sigma_{1}

(or of
\sigma_{2}
) to
\mathcal{G}^{+}
, and that extension satisfies
\pi\simeq i^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\sigma_{1})\simeq i^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}_{\mathcal{G}}(\sigma_{2})

Remark 2.6
.

Recall
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})\rtimes\Gamma\simeq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})
.
Denote by
{{\gamma}^{*}}V
the
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})
module with the same underlying vector space but with the action twisted by
{\gamma}
. From (
2.3
) and (
2.4
), we have

(2.17)

\displaystyle(\gamma^{*}\pi)^{\mathcal{J}}\simeq{\gamma}^{*}(\pi^{\mathcal{J}})

Hence from
[
24
]
, we get the same result as above.

2.3.
Jacquet and induction functors

Recall
\mathbb{B}
denotes the Borel subgroup of upper triangular matrices. Denote by
\bar{\mathbb{B}}
the opposite Borel subgroup, i.e., the lower triangular matrices.
Let
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
be a parabolic subgroup of
\mathcal{G}
containing
\bar{\mathbb{B}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and normarlized by
\gamma
. Write
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{M}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
with Levi subgroup
\mathcal{M}
and unipotent radical
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. Then
\gamma
normalizes
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. Define
\mathcal{M}^{+}=\mathcal{M}\rtimes\Gamma
,
\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}=\mathcal{M}^{+}\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. Since
\mathcal{M}^{+}
normalizes
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
, we can define functors
i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
and
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
as in
[
4
, 1.8]
.

Recall that for any element
y\in Y^{+}
, we have
y\mapsto y(\varpi^{-1})
. Let
T_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}
be the characteristic function of the double coset
\mathcal{J}y(\varpi^{-1})\mathcal{J}
, and
\theta_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}=p^{-l(y)/2}T_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}=\operatorname{vol}(\mathcal{J}y(\varpi^{-1})\mathcal{J})^{-1/2}T_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}
in
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})
. Then
y(\varpi^{-1})
is positive relative to (
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}(F)
,
\mathcal{J}
), and
T_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}
is an invertible element of
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})
.

Let
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}=\mathcal{J}\cap\mathcal{M}
. Then we have the affine Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
of
\mathcal{M}
with respect to
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
. By
[
7
, 7.9]
, there is an injective homomorphism

(2.18)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}:\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})

such that for a simple reflection
s_{\alpha}\in W^{\mathcal{M}}\subset W^{\mathcal{G}}
:

(2.19)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(T_{s_{\alpha}}^{\mathcal{M}})=T_{s_{\alpha}}^{\mathcal{G}}

and for
y\in Y^{+}
:

(2.20)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(T_{y}^{\mathcal{M}})=\frac{\operatorname{vol}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}y(\varpi^{-1})\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})^{1/2}}{\operatorname{vol}(\mathcal{J}y(\varpi^{-1})\mathcal{J})^{1/2}}T_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}

i.e.

(2.21)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}(\theta_{y}^{\mathcal{M}})=\theta_{y}^{\mathcal{G}}

Then we obtain an injective homomorphism:

(2.22)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}^{0}:\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})

via the embedding
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})\simeq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})\rtimes\Gamma
. Consequently, there is a
\Gamma
-action on
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
. Using the same method as before, we also have an embedding
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
,
which induces another
\Gamma
-action on the same algebra. One easily checks that these two
\Gamma
-actions coincide. Hence we obtain an injective homomorphism:

(2.23)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}:\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})

satisfying the same relations (
2.19
) and (
2.21
) as above, together with

(2.24)

t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}})=T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}

where
T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}}
is the characteristic function of the double coset
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
and
T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}
is the characteristic function of the double coset
\mathcal{J}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}
.

For
\mathcal{M}^{+}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M}^{+})
whose restriction to
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M})
lies in
\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
.
By the same arguments as in Propositions
2.3
and Propositions
2.4
, we obtain an equivalence of categories

(2.25)

\displaystyle{\Lambda^{+}_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}}:\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})-\mathrm{Mod}

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle\mapsto V^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}

Proposition 2.7
.

There is a commutative diagram:

(2.26)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})-\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda^{+}_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})-\mathrm{Mod}}

where the right vertical restriction is via (
2.23
)
t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}:\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\to\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})

Proof.

Let
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
, and write
(\pi_{u},V_{u})
for the Jacquet module of
(\pi,V)
relative to the Jacquet functor
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
.
From the definition of the Jacquet functor,
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}V
and
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}V
have the same underlying space. Let
q
denote the quotient map
V\to V_{u}
.

From the proof in
[
7
, 7.9]
. We have
q(V^{\mathcal{J}})=V_{u}^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}
.
We define a map:

(2.27)

\displaystyle q:V^{\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\to V_{u}^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto q(v)

Hence
q
is an isomorphism, and for
v\in V^{\mathcal{J}}
,
f\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
, we have

(2.28)

\displaystyle q(\pi(t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime}}f)v)=\pi_{u}(f)q(v)

Now let
f=T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}}
. We have

\displaystyle\pi_{u}(T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}})\cdot q(v)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}}(h)\pi_{u}(h)\cdot q(v)dh

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}}(h)q(\pi(h)v)dh

\displaystyle=q\circ\int_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}}(h)\pi(h)\cdot vdh

\displaystyle=q\circ\int_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}\pi(h)\cdot vdh

Denote
\mathcal{J}_{u}=\mathcal{J}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}_{u}=\mathcal{J}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{op}}
where
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{op}}
is opposite of
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. We have

\displaystyle\pi(t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}})v

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(h)\pi(h)\cdot vdh

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{J}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(h)\pi(h)\cdot vdh

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{u}\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{J}_{l}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(h_{u}h_{\mathcal{M}}h_{l})\pi(h_{u}h_{\mathcal{M}}h_{l})\cdot vdh_{l}dh_{\mathcal{M}}dh_{u}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{J}_{u}}\int_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}(1,\gamma)\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}\pi(h)\cdot vdhdh_{u}

We apply
q
to this equation. Combining (
2.28
), for
f\in\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
, we obtain

q(\pi(t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}f)v)=\pi_{u}(f)q(v)

∎

Let
\mathcal{P}
denote the opposite of
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, which contains
{\mathbb{B}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(the upper triangular Borel subgroup) and
{\mathcal{M}}
. Define
\mathcal{P}^{+}=\mathcal{P}\rtimes\Gamma
. Then from
[
18
, 5.5]
:

Proposition 2.8

(
[
18
]
)
.

If
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})
is admissible, the contragredient of
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi)
is isomorphic to
r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\tilde{\pi})
as
\mathcal{M}^{+}
-representations, i.e.

\widetilde{r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi)}\simeq r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\tilde{\pi})
, where
\widetilde{(\cdot)}
denotes the contragredient representation.

Remark 2.9
.

In fact, the above isomorphism holds more generally, not only for admissible representations. Using the
\mathcal{M}
-equivariant non‑degenerate pairing introduced in
[
6
]
(see also
[
25
]
or
[
8
]
for further details), one notes that the original pairing is
\mathcal{G}^{+}
-equivariant. Choosing a
\gamma
-invariant open compact subgroup, the pairing becomes
\mathcal{M}^{+}
-equivariant. In our case
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
, it can be described as
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
-equivariant via
{\Lambda^{+}_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}}
for simplicity.

Proposition 2.10
.

If
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
, the contragredient of
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi)
is isomorphic to
r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\tilde{\pi})
as
\mathcal{M}^{+}
-representations, i.e.

\widetilde{r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi)}\simeq r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\tilde{\pi})
, where
\widetilde{(\cdot)}
denotes the contragredient representation.

Proof.

If
(\pi,V)\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
, we have
(\widetilde{\pi},\widetilde{V})\in\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}
and a canonical pairing:

(2.29)

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}\times V

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle(\tilde{v},v)

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\tilde{v},v\rangle

From
[
8
]
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{v},\pi(f)v\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\pi}(\tilde{f})\tilde{v},v\rangle

where
\tilde{f}:g\mapsto f(g^{-1})
,
g\in\mathcal{G}^{+}
, and
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
-invariant nondegenerate bilinear form

(2.30)

\displaystyle\tilde{V}_{l}^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}\times V_{u}^{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}\to\mathbb{C}

where
(\tilde{\pi}_{l},\tilde{V}_{l})
is the Jacquet module of
(\tilde{\pi},\tilde{V})
relative to the Jacquet functor
r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}
. The nondegenerate bilinear form (
2.30
) comes from (
2.29
) and (
2.27
). Since we have

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{v},\pi(t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}})v\rangle=\langle\widetilde{\pi}(t_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}T_{\gamma}^{\mathcal{M}^{+}})\tilde{v},v\rangle

and (
2.28
), the nondegenerate bilinear form (
2.30
) is
\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
-invariant. Using the equivalence of categories (
2.25
), we obtain the proposition.
∎

Then we have Bernstein’s second adjointness theorem.

Theorem 2.11
.

The functor
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
is right adjoint to
i_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}
, i.e.
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}(\pi_{1},r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi_{2}))\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(i_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\pi_{1}),\pi_{2})

Proof.

Using the same argument as in Bernstein and
[
4
, 1.9]
, we have
i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\tilde{\pi_{1}})=\widetilde{i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}({\pi_{1}})}
, and
r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
is left adjoint to
i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
. Then

(2.31)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi_{1}),\tilde{\pi_{2}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(\pi_{2},\widetilde{i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi_{1})})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{G}^{+}}(\pi_{2},{i_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\tilde{\pi_{1}}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}(r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi_{2}),{\tilde{\pi_{1}}})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}({{\pi_{1}}},\widetilde{r_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}(\pi_{2})})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}^{+}}({{\pi_{1}}},{r_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}(\tilde{\pi_{2}})})

If
\pi
is admissible, then
\pi\simeq\tilde{\tilde{\pi}}
and we get the theorem. For the general case, the same result follows by a diagram chase as in
[
6
]
.
∎

Hence, by uniqueness of adjoint functors, the following diagram commutes:

(2.32)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{G}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}}^{+}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})-\mathrm{Mod}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{M}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\Lambda^{+}_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}}}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})-\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})}(-)}

Remark 2.12
.

In the connected case,
{\mathcal{P}}
contains
{\mathbb{B}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(the upper triangular Borel subgroup), we can use diagram
2.32
to translate Bernstein-Zelevinsky classification into modules of the affine Hecke algebra.

3.
Representations of twisted affine Hecke algebra

Now we translate everything to the complex dual group and use the geometry of the twisted affine Hecke algebra and the twisted graded Hecke algebra to classify their irreducible modules and compute multiplicities.

3.1.
Twisted affine Hecke algebra

Let
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
be the dual group of
\mathcal{G}
. Define the complex dual group
G^{+}:=G\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
, where
\hat{\Gamma}=\langle\hat{\gamma}\rangle
for
\hat{\gamma}(g)=J(^{t}g^{-1})J^{-1}
. Let
W^{+}:=N_{G^{+}}(T)
. Then
W^{+}=W\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
. Denote the root datum of
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
by
\mathcal{R}(G,T)=(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{R},\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{R}^{\vee},\widetilde{\Pi})
. Then we have
\hat{\Gamma}
action on
W
and
\widetilde{X}
.

Definition 3.1
.

The twisted affine Hecke algebra
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
is the vector space
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]
with the following multiplication rules:

\bullet

\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]
,
\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
,
\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]
are embedded as subalgebras, where
v
is an indeterminate.

\bullet

(T_{s_{\alpha}}+1)(T_{s_{\alpha}}-v^{2})=0
,
\alpha\in\widetilde{\Pi}

\bullet

T_{w_{1}}T_{w_{2}}=T_{w_{1}w_{2}}
, if
l(w_{1}w_{2})=l(w_{1})+l(w_{2})
,
w_{1},w_{2}\in W

\bullet
For
x\in\widetilde{X}
and
\alpha\in\widetilde{\Pi}
:

(3.1)

\theta_{x}T_{s_{\alpha}}-T_{s_{\alpha}}\theta_{s_{\alpha}(x)}=(v^{2}-1)\frac{\theta_{x}-\theta_{s_{\alpha}(x)}}{1-\theta_{-\alpha}}

\bullet
For
T_{\gamma}\in\hat{\Gamma}
,
w\in W
, and
x\in\widetilde{X}
:

(3.2)

\displaystyle T_{\gamma}^{2}

\displaystyle=1

\displaystyle T_{\gamma}\theta_{x}T_{w}T_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\theta_{\gamma(x)}T_{\gamma(w)}

From the definition, we have
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
.

Since
\mathcal{R}(G,T)=\mathcal{R}^{\vee}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{T})=(Y,R^{\vee},X,R,\Pi^{\vee})=(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{R},\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{R}^{\vee},\widetilde{\Pi})
, setting
v=p^{1/2}
, from Proposition
2.1
there is an isomorphism

(3.3)

\mathcal{H}(G^{+},p^{1/2})\simeq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}^{+}/\mathcal{J})

.
Define
\mathcal{H}(G,v)=\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]
as a subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
. Then
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)=\mathcal{H}(G,v)\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
and
\mathcal{H}(G,p^{1/2})\simeq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}\backslash\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{J})
.

For a Levi subgroup
\mathcal{M}
normalized by
\gamma
, let
M
be a Levi subgroup of
{G}
that is the complex dual of
\mathcal{M}
. Then
\hat{\gamma}
normalizes
M
. Define
{M}^{+}={M}\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
. Denote by
W_{M}
the subgroup of
W
corresponding to
M
. Define
\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)=\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[W_{M}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]
, a subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
. Specializing
v=p^{1/2}
gives
\mathcal{H}(M^{+},p^{1/2})\simeq\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}\backslash\mathcal{M}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})
and the injective homomorphism (
2.23
)
t_{\mathcal{P}^{\prime+}}
is obtained by specializing the variable
v
to
p^{1/2}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

The algebra
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]^{W^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
is the center of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
.

Proof.

From
[
21
]
, the central subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
is
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]^{W}\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
. Taking into account the action of
\hat{\Gamma}
on
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
, we find that
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]^{W^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
is the center of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
.
∎

We view
\mathscr{A}=\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{X}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
as the ring of regular functions on
T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
, where
T\simeq\widetilde{Y}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{*}
is the algebraic torus with a natural action of
W^{+}
.

Let
\chi
be the central character of the center
\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}
for
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
given by the
W^{+}
-orbit
(W^{+}\cdot t,v_{0})
, where
(t,v_{0})\in T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
is semisimple.
By Schur’s lemma, we obtain a canonical partition
\mathrm{Irr}\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)=\sqcup\mathrm{Irr}_{W^{+}\cdot t,v_{0}}\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
obtained by specifying the action of the centre of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
on a simple
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
-module.

Let
L
be a finite dimensional left
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
module.
Define
\hat{\gamma}^{*}L
to be the same vector space
L
with the twisted action
h\cdot l=\hat{\gamma}(h)l
for
h\in\mathcal{H}(G,v)
and
l\in L
.

We define

(3.4)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{(t,v_{0})}^{+}=\{f\in\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}|f(t,v_{0})=0\}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{(t,v_{0})}=\{f\in\mathscr{A}^{W}|f(t,v_{0})=0\}

Denote by
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
) the category of finite dimensional modules of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
(resp.
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
) annihilated by some power of
\mathscr{I}_{(t,v_{0})}^{+}
(resp.
\mathscr{I}_{(t,v_{0})}
).

We denote by
\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}_{({t},v_{0})}}
the
\mathscr{I}_{({t},v_{0})}^{+}
-adic completion of
\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}
and
\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W}_{({t},v_{0})}}
the
\mathscr{I}_{({t},v_{0})}
-adic completion of
\mathscr{A}^{W}
.

We define

(3.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}}\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}^{W}}\mathcal{H}(G,v)

If
\hat{\Gamma}\cdot t\not\subseteq G\cdot t
, take
t^{\prime}=\hat{\gamma}\cdot t
.

Lemma 3.3
.

The functor
L\mapsto\hat{\gamma}^{*}L
is an equivalence between the categories
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
and
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t^{\prime},v_{0})}
.

Proof.

For
f\in\mathscr{A}^{W}
,
l\in\hat{\gamma}^{*}L
, we have action
f\cdot l=\hat{\gamma}(f)l=f(\hat{\gamma}((t,v_{0})))l=f((t^{\prime},v_{0}))l
. Moreover, there is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
-module
\hat{\gamma}^{*}(\hat{\gamma}^{*}L)\simeq L
, and we obtain the lemma.
∎

Theorem 3.4
.

For a simple module
N\in\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
, the restriction
\mathrm{Res}^{\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)}_{\mathcal{H}(G,v)}(N)=L_{1}\oplus L_{2}
, where
L_{1}
,
L_{2}
are simple modules of
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
,
L_{1}\in\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
,
L_{2}\in\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t^{\prime},v_{0})}
, and
L_{2}\simeq\hat{\gamma}^{*}L_{1}
. Hence
N\simeq\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)}_{\mathcal{H}(G,v)}(L_{1})\simeq\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)}_{\mathcal{H}(G,v)}(L_{2})
.

Proof.

For a simple module
N\in\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
, let
L
be a simple
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
submodule of
N
. Let
\chi
denote the character of the center
\mathscr{A}^{W}
on the simple module
L
. Hence for
f\in\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}
, we have
\chi(f)=f((t,v_{0}))
. As a result, the central character
\chi
is given by the
W
-orbit
(W\cdot t,v_{0})
or the
W
-orbit
(W\cdot t^{\prime},v_{0})
. The simple
\mathcal{H}(G,v)
-submodule
L
lies either in
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
or in
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t^{\prime},v_{0})}
. From
[
24
, ThmA.6]
and Lemma
3.3
, we obtain the theorem.
∎

In fact, we can obtain a finer description of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}
and its module category.

Lemma 3.5
.

The algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}
is isomorphic to
M_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})})
, the
2\times 2
matrix algebra with coefficients in
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})}
.

Proof.

We define

\displaystyle\mathscr{Z}_{(t,v_{0})}=\{f\in\mathscr{A}|f(t,v_{0})=0\}\quad\text{a maximal ideal of $\mathscr{A}$}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathscr{Z}_{(t,v_{0})}}=\text{$\mathscr{Z}_{(t,v_{0})}$-adic completion of $\mathscr{A}$}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{+}_{({t},v_{0})}}=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}}\mathscr{A}

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}_{({t},v_{0})}}=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}^{W}}\mathscr{A}

We obtain an isomorphism of algebra

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{+}_{({t},v_{0})}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{s\in W^{+}\cdot t}\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathscr{Z}_{(s,v_{0})}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{s\in W\cdot t}\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathscr{Z}_{(s,v_{0})}}\bigoplus_{s\in W\cdot t^{\prime}}\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathscr{Z}_{(s,v_{0})}}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{A}_{({t},v_{0})}}\bigoplus\widehat{\mathscr{A}_{({t^{\prime}},v_{0})}}

and

(3.6)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}^{W^{+}}}\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathscr{A}^{+}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]

\displaystyle=(\widehat{\mathscr{A}_{({t},v_{0})}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}])\bigoplus(\widehat{\mathscr{A}_{({t^{\prime}},v_{0})}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}])

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]\bigoplus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t^{\prime},v_{0})}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\hat{\Gamma}]

We define a homomorphism

\displaystyle M_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})})

\displaystyle\to\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{11}&f_{12}\\
f_{21}&f_{22}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\mapsto f_{11}+f_{12}\cdot(1,\hat{\gamma})+(1,\hat{\gamma})\cdot f_{21}+(1,\hat{\gamma})\cdot f_{22}\cdot(1,\hat{\gamma})

Since for
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})}
, we have
\hat{\gamma}(f)\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t^{\prime},v_{0})}
,
(1,\hat{\gamma})\cdot f=\hat{\gamma}(f)\cdot(1,\hat{\gamma})
and
(1,\hat{\gamma})\cdot f\cdot(1,\hat{\gamma})=\hat{\gamma}(f)
. Hence there is an isomorphism
M_{2}(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})})\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}
and this is compatible with the ring structures, and we obtain the lemma.
∎

Theorem 3.6
.

There is an equivalence of categories:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(G,v)}L

Proof.

From Lemma
3.5
,
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)_{(t,v_{0})}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)_{(t,v_{0})}
are Morita equivalent. In fact, it is given by
L\mapsto\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(G,v)}L
from
\mathcal{H}(G,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
to
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,v_{0})}
.
∎

3.2.
Twisted graded Hecke algebra

Let
G^{+}
be a complex reductive algebraic group with the identity component
G
. Let
B=TU
be a Borel subgroup of
G
with Levi factor
T
and unipotent radical
U
, and
\mathfrak{t}=\mathrm{Lie}(T)
. We denote the root datum by
\mathcal{R}(G,T)
. Let
W=N_{G}(T)/T
,
W^{+}=N_{G^{+}}(T)/T
,
\mathfrak{R}=N_{G^{+}}(B,T)/T
where
N_{G^{+}}(B,T)=N_{G^{+}}(B)\cap N_{G^{+}}(T)
, hence
W^{+}=W\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
,
W
is Weyl group of
\mathcal{R}(G,T)
.
Let
\mathfrak{r}
be an indeterminate, identified with the coordinate function on
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
be the dual space of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}=\mathrm{Lie}(T)
and
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})
be the symmetric algebra of
\mathfrak{t}^{*}
.

Proposition 3.7
.

[
1
, 2.2]

There exists a unique associative algebra structure on
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}[W^{+}]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
such that

\bullet
the twisted group algebra
\mathbb{C}[W^{+}]
is embedded as subalgebra

\bullet
the algebra
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
of polynomial functions on
\mathfrak{t}\oplus\mathbb{C}
is embedded as a subalgebra.

\bullet

\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
is central

\bullet

N_{\alpha}\xi-s_{\alpha}(\xi)N_{\alpha}=2\mathfrak{r}(\xi-s_{\alpha}(\xi))/\alpha
for
\xi\in S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})
and simple roots
\alpha
.

\bullet

N_{\tau}\xi N_{\tau}^{-1}=\tau(\xi)
for
\xi\in S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})
and
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}
.

We denote the algebra of Proposition
3.7
by
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})
. Define
\mathbb{H}(G)=S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}[W]\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
as a subalgebra of
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})
; then
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})=\mathbb{H}(G)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
.

Lemma 3.8
.

The algebra
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
is the center of
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})
.

Proof.

By a result of Lusztig, the center of
\mathbb{H}(G)
is
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
. Taking into account the action of
\mathfrak{R}
on
\mathbb{H}(G)
, we obtain that
S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
is the center of
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})
.
∎

Now we return to our specific case
G^{+}=G\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
. Let
t
be a semisimple element in
G
such that
\hat{\Gamma}\cdot t\subseteq G\cdot t
. Let
t=t_{c}t_{h}
be its polar decomposition, where
t_{c}=t|t|^{-1}
is the compact part and
t_{h}=|t|
is the hyperbolic part. Then
Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c})^{0}
has root datum
(\widetilde{X},R_{t_{c}},\widetilde{Y},R_{t_{c}}^{\vee},\Pi_{t_{c}})
and we have a graded Hecke algebra
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))
.

From now on, assume
v_{0}>0
. Denote by
\bar{\chi}
the central character of the center of
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))
given by the
(W^{+}_{t_{c}}\cdot\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}v_{0})
, and denote by
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{\bar{\chi}}
the category of finite dimensional modules of
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))
on which the center acts via the central character
\bar{\chi}
.

Theorem 3.9
.

[
[
2
]
]
There is an equivalence
\Theta
between the categories
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{\chi}
and
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{\bar{\chi}}
, where
\chi
is the central character of
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)
given by
(W^{+}\cdot t,v_{0})
and
\bar{\chi}
is the central character of
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))
given by the
(W^{+}_{t_{c}}\cdot\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}v_{0})
.

The functor
\Theta
is compatible with parabolic induction, in the following sense. let
M
be a Levi subgroup of
{G}
which is normalized by
\hat{\gamma}
, and define
{M}^{+}={M}\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
. Then
\Theta\circ\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)}^{\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{H}(Z_{M^{+}}(t_{c}))}^{\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))}\circ\Theta
, where
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)}^{\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)}
(resp.
\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{H}(Z_{M^{+}}(t_{c}))}^{\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))}
) is given by
\mathcal{H}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)}(-)
(resp.
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))\otimes_{\mathbb{H}(Z_{M^{+}}(t_{c}))}(-)
).

Remark 3.10
.

For a semisimple element
t\in\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
satisfying
\hat{\Gamma}\cdot t\subseteq G\cdot t
, the short exact sequence

(3.7)

1\to Z_{G}(t_{c})\to Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c})\to\hat{\Gamma}_{t_{c}}\to 1

oes not split in some cases. However, there is always a homomorphism
\hat{\Gamma}_{t_{c}}\to\mathrm{Out}(Z_{G}(t_{c}))\simeq\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{B}(Z_{G}(t_{c})))

[
15
]
, which induces an isomorphism
\hat{\Gamma}_{t_{c}}\simeq\mathfrak{R}
. Consequently, we obtain
\mathbb{H}(Z_{G^{+}}(t_{c}))=\mathbb{H}(Z_{G}(t_{c}))\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
. We will discuss this in more detail in the section of Whittaker normalization.

4.
Representations of twisted graded Hecke algebra

We use a superscript
*
to denote the dual of a vector space or of a local system. If a group
G
acts on a vector space
V
, we denote the space of
G
-invariant vectors by
V^{G}
. We write
S(V)=\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}S^{j}(V)
for the symmetric algebra of
V
.

4.1.
Equivariant derived category

Let
A
be a complex linear algebraic group and let
X
be an
A
-variety, that is a quasiprojective complex algebraic variety with an algebraic
A
-action. We consider sheaves of
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces on X with respect to the analytic topology. We denote by
D(X)
the bounded derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
and
D_{A}(X)
the
A
-equivariant bounded derived category as defined in
[
5
]
. This is a triangulated category with a
t
-structure and there is a forgetful functor
\operatorname{For}:D_{A}(X)\to D(X)
.

We now recall the construction
D_{A}(X)
. Let
I\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite interval, and let
k\geq|I|
. Let
Sf_{k}(A)
be the category whose objects are smooth free
A
-varieties
Z_{k}
that are
k
-acyclic
and whose morphisms are smooth free
A
-morphisms. For a chosen
Z_{k}\in Sf_{k}(A)
, we have the diagram
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{k}}}{{\longleftarrow}}X\times Z_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\backslash(X\times Z_{k})
. An object
\mathcal{I}
in
D_{A}^{I}(X,Z_{k})
consists of a triple
(\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{k},\beta)
with
\mathcal{I}_{0}\in D^{I}(X)
,
\mathcal{I}_{k}\in D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}))
, and an isomorphism
\beta:p_{k}^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{0})\simeq q_{k}^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{k})
.

A morphism
\alpha:(\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{k},\beta_{I})\mapsto(\mathcal{H}_{0},\mathcal{H}_{k},\beta_{H})
in
D^{I}_{A}(X,Z_{k})
is a pair
\alpha=(\alpha_{X},\bar{\alpha})
where
\alpha_{X}:\mathcal{I}_{0}\mapsto\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
\bar{\alpha}:\mathcal{I}_{k}\mapsto\mathcal{H}_{k}
satisfy
\beta_{H}\cdot p_{k}^{*}(\alpha_{X})=q_{k}^{*}(\bar{\alpha})\cdot\beta_{I}

For another object
Z_{k^{\prime}}\in Sf_{k^{\prime}}(A)
with
k^{\prime}\geq|I|
, there is a canonical equivalence of categories
D^{I}_{A}(X,Z_{k})\simeq D^{I}_{A}(X,Z_{k^{\prime}})
. This yields a well defined category
D^{I}_{A}(X)
. Finally we define
D_{A}(X)=\lim_{I}D^{I}_{A}(X)
.

Remark 4.1
.

Let
A\subseteq\mathrm{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})
be a closed subgroup, and let
V_{k}
be the Stiefel variety of
n
-tuples of linearly independent vectors in
\mathbb{C}^{n+k+1}
. Then
V_{k}\in Sf_{k}(A)
.

Remark 4.2
.

In fact, as explained in
[
5
, 2.3.2]
, for a given
Z_{k}\in Sf_{k}(A)
with
k\geq|I|
, we can recover
D^{I}_{A}(X)
from a full subcategory of
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}))
.

Let
X
,
Y
be
A
-varieties and let
f:X\to Y
be an
A
-morphism. Then we have functors:

f^{*},f^{!}:D_{A}(Y)\to D_{A}(X)\quad f_{*},f_{!}:D_{A}(X)\to D_{A}(Y)

If
A^{\prime}\hookrightarrow A
is a subgroup, then an
A
-variety
X
can also be regarded as an
A^{\prime}
-variety, and there is a restriction functor
\operatorname{Res}^{A}_{A^{\prime}}:D_{A}(X)\to D_{A^{\prime}}(X)
. The four functors above commute with the restriction functor
\mathrm{Res}^{A}_{A^{\prime}}
in the sense that there are canonical isomorphisms
\mathrm{Res}^{A}_{A^{\prime}}\cdot f^{*}\simeq f^{*}\cdot\mathrm{Res}^{A}_{A^{\prime}}
, and similarly for
f^{!}
,
f_{*}
,
f_{!}
.

We have the functor
\otimes
and
R\mathscr{Hom}
in the equivariant case.
We also have the equivariant dualizing complex
\omega_{X}=a_{X}^{!}\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{pt}}

(a_{X}:X\to\mathrm{pt})
and Verdier duality
D(\mathcal{I}):=R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{I},\omega_{X})
. The following natural functorial isomorphisms hold:

(4.1)

\displaystyle R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{I}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{2},\mathcal{I}_{3})

\displaystyle\simeq R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{I}_{1},R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{I}_{2},\mathcal{I}_{3}))

\displaystyle f^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{2})

\displaystyle\simeq f^{*}\mathcal{I}_{1}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle D\cdot f^{*}

\displaystyle\simeq f^{!}\cdot D

\displaystyle D\cdot f_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}\cdot D

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, define a functor
H^{j}_{A}(X,-)
from
D_{A}(X)
to the category of finite dimensional
\mathbb{C}
-vector spaces. Let
\mathcal{I}=(\mathcal{I}_{0},\mathcal{I}_{k},\beta_{I})\in D_{A}(X)
. Choose
k\geq j
, and set

\displaystyle H^{j}_{A}(X,\mathcal{I}):=H^{j}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),\mathcal{I}_{k})

The definition is independent of the choices. Moreover, if
f:X\to Y
is a
A
-morphism, then
H^{j}_{A}(X,\mathcal{I})=H^{j}_{A}(Y,f_{*}\mathcal{I})
.

We define the equivariant homology by

\displaystyle H_{j}^{A}(X,\mathcal{I})=H_{A}^{j-2\mathrm{dim}X}(X,\mathcal{I}\otimes\omega_{X}))

Let
\mathcal{L}
be an
A
-equivariant local system on
X
. We have
\mathcal{L}^{*}=R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{L},\mathbb{C}_{X})
. From (
4.1
), we have
R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{L\otimes\omega}_{X},\omega_{X})\simeq R\mathscr{H}om(\mathcal{L},\mathbb{C}_{X})
. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{L\otimes\omega}_{X}\simeq D(\mathcal{L}^{*})

Hence

\displaystyle H_{j}^{A}(X,\mathcal{L})=H_{A}^{j-2\mathrm{dim}X}(X,D(\mathcal{L}^{*}))

For
\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\in D_{A}(X)
, we define

(4.2)

\oplus_{j+j^{\prime}=i}H^{j}_{A}(X,\mathcal{I})\times H^{j^{\prime}}_{A}(X,\mathcal{I}^{\prime})\to H^{i}_{A}(X,\mathcal{I}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{\prime})

It is given by the cup product
H^{j}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),\mathcal{I}_{k})\times H^{j^{\prime}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{k})\to H^{i}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),\mathcal{I}_{k}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{k})
with large enough
k
.

We have canonically

(4.3)

\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{j}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{I}^{\prime})=H^{j}_{A}(X,D(D\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}))

and

H^{j}_{A}(X,D(D\mathcal{I}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{\prime}))=H^{j}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),D(D(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{k})\otimes\mathcal{I}_{k}))=\mathrm{Hom}(H_{c}^{-j}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}),D(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{k})\otimes\mathcal{I}_{k}),\mathbb{C})
.

Using (
4.2
), we note that
H^{*}_{A}(X,D(D\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}))
is a
H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})
-module.

Taking
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{X}
in (
4.3
), we deduce

(4.4)

\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{j}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})=H^{j}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})

Hence
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{I}^{\prime})
can be viewed as
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
-module as follows: the product of
\phi:\mathbb{C}_{X}\to\mathbb{C}_{X}[n]
with
\psi:\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{I}^{\prime}[m]
is
\phi\otimes\psi:\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{I}^{\prime}[n+m]
using
\mathcal{I}=\mathbb{C}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
. Then the isomorphism (
4.3
) is an isomorphism of
H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})
-modules, where the left-hand side is a module via the identification
H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})\cong\operatorname{Hom}^{*}_{D_{A}(X)}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
and the right-hand side via the cup product.

Let
A^{+}
be an algebraic group, and let
A
be a connected normal subgroup with
\mathfrak{R}:=A^{+}/A=\langle 1,\tau\rangle
. For an
A^{+}
-variety
X
, we have a functor
D_{A^{+}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}D_{A}(X)
.

We define the functor
\tau^{*}
on
D_{A}(X)
. Take a representative
\bar{\tau}
of
\tau
in
A^{+}
. Since
X
is
A^{+}
-variety,
\bar{\tau}:X\to X
is twisted
A
-equivariant, i.e.,
\bar{\tau}(ax)=\mathrm{Ad}_{\bar{\tau}}(a)\bar{\tau}(x)
where
a\in A
,
x\in X
. Take
Z_{k}^{+}\in Sf_{k}(A^{+})
, hence for
a\in A
,
z\in Z_{k}^{+}
we have
{\bar{\tau}}(az)=\mathrm{Ad}_{\bar{\tau}}(a)\bar{\tau}(z)
. Then we have
\bar{\tau}
action on
X\times Z_{k}
which is twisted
A
-equivariant. It induces a
\bar{\tau}
action on
A\backslash(X\times Z_{k})
. If we change another representative
\bar{\tau}^{\prime}
, we have
\bar{\tau}^{\prime}\cdot\bar{\tau}^{-1}=a\in A
, hence
\bar{\tau}^{\prime}\cdot\bar{\tau}^{-1}
induces the identity map on
A\backslash(X\times Z_{k})
, therefore the action
\bar{\tau}:A\backslash(X\times Z_{k})\to A\backslash(X\times Z_{k})
does not depend on the choice of representative. Finally this
\bar{\tau}
defines a functor
\tau^{*}
on
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}))
by pullback the complexes via
\bar{\tau}
action on the space. From
[
5
, 6.5]
, it means we can always find a
k
-acyclic compatible resolution of
\bar{\tau}
, hence
\tau^{*}
is well defined on
D_{A}(X)
.

Lemma 4.3
.

There is an algebraic isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}^{*}_{D_{A^{+}}(X)}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))^{\tau}

Proof.

Take
Z_{k}^{+}\in Sf_{k}(A^{+})
, then
Z_{k}^{+}\in Sf_{k}(A)
and we have the projection
\pi:A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})\to A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})
. We have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{D^{I}_{A^{+}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}}
\textstyle{D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{D^{I}_{A}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}

Let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D_{A^{+}}(X)
and consider a morphism

f\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
.
Via the bottom horizontal embedding,
f
corresponds to a morphism

f_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{*}_{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})
for any
I
.

Since we have an action
\tau
on
A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})
by left multiplication, we have
\tau^{*}
action on
\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})
:

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\tau^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\tau^{*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})

where the second identity is induced by
\pi\circ\tau=\pi
.

Since
\pi:A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})\to A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})
is the quotient by
\mathfrak{R}
and
\mathfrak{R}
action on
A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})
is free, we have a canonical equivalence
D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\simeq D^{I}_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))
. Since
\mathfrak{R}
is discrete group, we have a canonical equivalence
D^{I}_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\simeq D^{I}(Sh_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})))
. Therefore
\pi^{*}
induces the equivalence of categories:

D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\simeq D^{I}(Sh_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})))

We define the category
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}
of
\mathfrak{R}
-equivariant objects in
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))
as follows: an object in
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}
is a pair
(\mathcal{I},\kappa_{\tau})
where
\mathcal{I}\in D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))
and
\kappa_{\tau}:\mathcal{I}\to\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}
is an isomorphism satisfying
\kappa_{\tau}\circ\tau^{*}(\kappa_{\tau})=1
; a morphism in
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}
is a morphism of
D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))
,
\phi:\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{*}(\phi)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{\prime}_{\tau}}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

There is natural functor
D^{I}(Sh_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})))\to D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))
.
By
[
13
]
, this functor is an equivalence of categories
D^{I}(Sh_{\mathfrak{R}}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+})))\simeq D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}
.
Equivalently, by
[
12
]
, the pullback functor
\pi^{*}
directly gives an equivalence
D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\simeq D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}=\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}[j]
, then
\kappa_{\tau}
and
\kappa^{\prime}_{\tau}
are identity map. Let
f_{k}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}[j])=\mathrm{Hom}^{j}_{D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}(\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}_{k},\pi^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})
, then
\pi^{*}(f)
induces the algebraic isomorphism
\mathrm{Hom}^{*}_{D_{A^{+}}(X)}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathrm{Hom}^{*}_{D_{A}(X)}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}))^{\tau}
.
∎

In fact, we can define a descent datum to recover
D_{A^{+}}(X)
inside
D_{A}(X)
.

From the definition of the functor
\tau^{*}
, we have
\tau^{*}\circ\tau^{*}=1
. We define the category
D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}
as follows: an object in
D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}
is a pair
(\mathcal{I},\kappa_{\tau})
where
\mathcal{I}\in D_{A}(X)
and
\kappa_{\tau}:\mathcal{I}\to\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}
is an isomorphism satisfying
\kappa_{\tau}\circ\tau^{*}(\kappa_{\tau})=1
; a morphism in
D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}
is a morphism of
D_{A}(X)
,
\phi:\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{\tau}}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{*}(\phi)}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa^{\prime}_{\tau}}
\textstyle{\tau^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}}

The shift functor and distinguished triangles on
D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}
are defined as in
D_{A}(X)
.

Theorem 4.4
.

The functor

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}}:D_{A^{+}}(X)\to D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}

is an equivalence of categories. The functor
{\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}}
is a triangulated functor, and
D_{A}(X)^{\mathfrak{R}}
is a triangulated category.

Proof.

We use the notation and the proof in Lemma
4.3
. The category
D_{A^{+}}(X)
is the pullback of the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{D^{I}_{A^{+}}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}}
\textstyle{D^{I}(A^{+}\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))\simeq D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))^{\mathfrak{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}}
\textstyle{D^{I}_{A}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{I}(A\backslash(X\times Z_{k}^{+}))}

The statement then follows from the definition and Remark
4.2
.
∎

Let
f_{1}
,
f_{2}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
. Because
\tau^{*}
is a functor, we have
\tau^{*}(f_{1}\circ f_{2})=\tau^{*}(f_{1})\circ\tau^{*}(f_{2})
. Hence the
\tau
-action on
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
defined in (
4.5
) satisfies
\tau(f_{1}\cdot f_{2})=\tau(f_{1})\cdot\tau(f_{2})
.

Taking
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{X}
, we obtain a
\tau
-action on
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
and
\tau(g_{1}\cdot g_{2})=\tau(g_{1})\cdot\tau(g_{2})
for
g_{1}
,
g_{2}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
.

For any
g\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
,
f\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
, we have
\tau^{*}(g\otimes f)=\tau^{*}(g)\otimes\tau^{*}(f)
. Consequently, the
\tau
-action is compatible with
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
-module structure on
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
which means that
\tau(g\cdot f)=\tau(g)\cdot\tau(f)
.

For
H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})
, we can define a
\tau
-action via the homomorphism
\tau^{*}:H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})\to H^{*}_{A}(X,\tau^{*}\mathbb{C})
.

Note that
\tau^{*}
sends
\phi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
to
\tau^{*}(\phi)\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X},\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})
.Taking
p:X\to\mathrm{pt}
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X},\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\tau_{*}\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(p^{*}\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{pt}},\tau_{*}\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(\mathrm{pt})}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{pt}},p_{*}\tau_{*}\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})

(4.6)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}_{A}(X,\tau^{*}\mathbb{C}_{X})

Therefore (
4.4
)
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})=H^{*}_{A}(X,\mathbb{C})
is compatible with the
\tau
-action.

Let
A
be a reductive group. We write
H_{A}^{*}
instead of
H_{A}^{*}(\mathrm{point})
where the point is regarded as an
A
-variety in the obvious way. From
[
20
, 1.11]
, we have:

H_{A}^{*}=S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}

where
\mathfrak{a}
is the Lie algebra of
A
, and
S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}
denotes the
A
-invariant subalgebra of the symmetric algebra
S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})
. For any semisimple element
s\in\mathfrak{a}
, we have the algebra homomorphism
\chi_{s}:S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})\to\mathbb{C}
given by evaluating a polynomial function at
s
. We denote
\mathbb{C}_{s}
as
H^{*}_{A}
-algebra via the algebra homomorphism
\chi_{s}:H_{A}^{*}\to\mathbb{C}
.

Recall that
A
is a connected normal subgroup of
A^{+}
with
\mathfrak{R}:=A^{+}/A=\langle 1,\tau\rangle
. From (
4.6
), we have the
\tau
-action on
H^{*}_{A}\simeq S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}
. Let
T_{A}
be a maximal torus of
A
, with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{t}_{A}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{a}
. Then the diagram

(4.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}_{A}\simeq S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}_{A}\simeq S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(\mathfrak{t}_{A}^{*})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S(\mathfrak{t}_{A}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d)}

is commutative, where the left vertical map is induced by the natural map
\mathfrak{t}_{A}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{a}
and
(d)
is induced by
H^{*}_{T_{A}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{*}_{T_{A}}
which is
S(\mathfrak{t}_{A}^{*})\to S(\mathfrak{t}_{A}^{*})
induced by the adjoint action
\mathfrak{R}
on
\mathfrak{t}_{A}
.

We have an algebra homomorphism
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(\mathrm{pt})}^{*}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C})\to\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathbb{C}_{X},\mathbb{C}_{X})
induced by
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{pt}
, and since
p^{*}\circ\tau^{*}=\tau^{*}\circ p^{*}
, this algebra homomorphism is compatible with the
\tau
-action.

Note that
H^{*}_{A}\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(\mathrm{pt})}^{*}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C})
, hence combining all above we get a
H^{*}_{A}
-module structure on
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
and

Proposition 4.5
.

The
\tau
action on
H^{*}_{A}
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
and the
H^{*}_{A}
-module structure of
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
are compatible. Explicitly, for all
h_{1},h_{2},h\in H^{*}_{A}

f_{1},f_{2},f\in\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))
, we have
\tau(f_{1}\cdot f_{2})=\tau(f_{1})\cdot\tau(f_{2})
,
\tau(h_{1}\cdot h_{2})=\tau(h_{1})\cdot\tau(h_{2})
,
\tau(h\cdot f)=\tau(h)\tau(f)
, where
\tau
action is given by (
4.7
) and (
4.3
).

4.2.
Geometry of graded Hecke algebra

We preserve the setup of section
3.2
. Let
G^{+}
be a complex reductive algebraic group with identity component
G
. Let
B=TU
be a Borel subgroup of
G
with Levi factor
T
and unipotent radical
U
, and
\mathfrak{t}=\mathrm{Lie}(T)
. We denote the root datum by
\mathcal{R}(G,T)
. Let
W=N_{G}(T)/T
,
W^{+}=N_{G^{+}}(T)/T
,
\mathfrak{R}=N_{G^{+}}(B,T)/T
, hence
W^{+}=W\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
, and
W
is the Weyl group of
\mathcal{R}(G,T)
. Finally, we have a twisted graded Hecke algebra
\mathbb{H}(G^{+})
.

Consider the variety

\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}=\{(x,gB)\in\mathfrak{g}\times G^{+}/B:\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})x\in\mathfrak{b}\}

We have a natural
G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
:

(4.8)

\displaystyle(g_{1},\lambda):(x,gB)\mapsto(\lambda^{-2}\mathrm{Ad}(g_{1})x,g_{1}gB)

Let
\mathbf{E}
be the set of all isomorphism classes of
G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant line bundles over
G^{+}/B
.

Lemma 4.6
.

There is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathbf{E}\simeq\mathfrak{t}^{*}\oplus\mathbb{C}

Proof.

We use the same method as in
[
22
, section8]
.
Clearly,
G^{+}/B
is a homogeneous variety of
G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
for the conjugation action. Then the stabilizer in
{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}
of
B\in G^{+}/B
is
B\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
. Hence
\mathbf{E}=\mathrm{Hom}(B\times\mathbb{C}^{*},\mathbb{C}^{*})=\mathrm{Hom}(T\times\mathbb{C}^{*},\mathbb{C}^{*})
. Finally, we get
\mathbb{C}\otimes\mathbf{E}\simeq\mathfrak{t}^{*}\oplus\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Let

We define a map

Remark 4.8
.

In the proof of the theorem, we use the result of
[
1
, (7)]
:

(4.18)

\mathrm{Hom}_{{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}}^{0}(K^{+},K^{+})\simeq\mathbb{C}[W^{+}]

Notice that it depends on the choice of isomorphism
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}\simeq\tau^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
(
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}
). But in our case
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
is the constant sheaf, so in fact we have a canonical choice, namely identity.

Combining Lemma
4.3
, we have

Theorem 4.9
.

There is an algebraic isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}(G^{+})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})^{\tau}

4.2.1.
Localization

From
[
22
, 1.20]
,
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
is naturally a
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}
-algebra. Concretely, there is a natural ring homomorphism
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
whose image is contained in the center of
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
.

Using Theorem
4.7
, we know that
Z(\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+}))=Z(\mathbb{H}(G^{+}))=S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})^{W^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
. The ring homomorphism
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
is obtained by composing the cup product action
\Delta
with the pullback map

H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}(\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+})
induced by the map
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\to\mathrm{point}
. From
[
1
, Proposition 3.5]
, since
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}(\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+})\simeq H_{T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}
, the map
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}(\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+})
is equivalent to the map
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to H_{T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}
induced by the inclusion
T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}\to G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
.

From
[
20
, 1.11]
,
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\simeq S(\mathfrak{g}^{*})^{G^{+}}\otimes\mathbb{C}[\mathfrak{r}]
,
H_{T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\simeq S(\mathfrak{t}^{*})\otimes\mathbb{C}
and the homomorphism
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to H_{T\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}
is induced by the natural map
\mathfrak{t}\to\mathfrak{g}
. Therefore, we have

Proposition 4.10

(
[
1
, Proposition 3.5]
)
.

The image of the ring homomorphism
H_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}^{*}\to\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
is precisely the center of
\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(\mathfrak{g})}^{*}(K^{+},K^{+})
.

Take
\bar{t}\in\mathfrak{t}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
r_{0}\in\mathbb{C}
,
a=(\bar{t},r_{0})
, and let
Z=Z_{G\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(a)
and
Z^{+}=Z_{G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}}(a)
.
Assume that
Z^{+}\neq Z
. Hence we have an exact sequence

1\to Z\to Z^{+}\to G^{+}/G\to 1

Recall that
G
is connected and
\mathfrak{R}=G^{+}/G=\langle 1,\tau\rangle
. Let
T_{a}
be the smallest torus in
G^{+}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
whose Lie algebra contains
a
. Then we denote by
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+,a}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{a}
the fixed point set of
T_{a}
on
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
and
\mathfrak{g}
, respectively. Thus,
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+,a}=\{(x,gB)\in\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}|[\bar{t},x]=2r_{0}x\}
,
{\mathfrak{g}}^{a}=\{x\in{\mathfrak{g}}|[\bar{t},x]=2r_{0}x\}
.

Then we have a
Z^{+}
-equivariant commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+,a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dot{j}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{f}^{+}}
\textstyle{\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{+}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}^{a}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}}

Define

(4.19)

\widetilde{K}^{+}:=\tilde{f}^{+}_{!}(\dot{j}^{+})^{*}\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}

i.e.
\widetilde{K}^{+}\in D_{Z^{+}}(\mathfrak{g}^{a})

Recall
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\times_{\mathfrak{g}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
, and let
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
be the restriction of
\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}\boxtimes(\dot{\mathcal{L}}^{+})^{*}
via the embedding
i:\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\hookrightarrow\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}\times\dot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
. Hence
\ddot{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
is the constant sheaf in our case.

Lemma 4.11
.

For constant sheaf on
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
, we have
H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+},\underline{\mathbb{C}})=0
.

Proof.

Denote
\dot{\mathfrak{g}}=\{(x,gB)\in\mathfrak{g}\times G/B:\mathrm{Ad}(g^{-1})x\in\mathfrak{b}\}
and
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}:=\dot{\mathfrak{g}}\times_{\mathfrak{g}}\dot{\mathfrak{g}}
.
Since
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+}
is a disjoint union of four copies of
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}
, we have
H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+},\mathbb{C})=\oplus_{i=1}^{4}H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbb{C})
, so it suffices to show
H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}},\mathbb{C})=0
.

For
w\in W
, set
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(w)}:=\{(x,gB,g^{\prime}B)\in\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}\mid g^{-1}g^{\prime}\in BwB\}
. Then
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}=\sqcup_{w\in W}\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(w)}
. Consider the projection

\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{pr}_{23}}}{{\longrightarrow}}G/B\times G/B

and denote
\mathcal{O}(w)=\mathrm{pr}_{23}\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(w)}
. Hence
\mathcal{O}(w)
is a
G
-orbit under the diagonal action and we have a poset of
G
-orbit closures in
G/B\times G/B
. Enumerate
\mathcal{O}(w)
in such an order:
\mathcal{O}(1)
,
\mathcal{O}(2)
,
\cdots
,
\mathcal{O}(m)
that
\mathrm{dim}\mathcal{O}(1)\geq\mathrm{dim}\mathcal{O}(2)\geq\cdots\mathrm{dim}\mathcal{O}(m)
. Let
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}=\sqcup_{i\geq j}\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(i)}
. Then
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{1}\supset\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}\supset\cdots\supset\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{m}
is a filtration over

\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{pr}_{3}}}{{\longrightarrow}}G/B

Since each
\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(w)}
is a vector bundle over
G/B
with restriction of
\mathrm{pr}_{3}
, we have

H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{\Omega(w)})=0

Using the open-closed long exact sequence for each
j
:

\displaystyle\cdots\to H_{c}^{i}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}-\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j+1})\to H_{c}^{i}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j})\to H_{c}^{i}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j+1})\to H_{c}^{i+1}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j}-\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}_{j+1})\to\cdots

We have
H_{c}^{\mathrm{odd}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{g}}^{+},\underline{\mathbb{C}})=0
by induction.
∎

Lemma
4.11
implies that the condition in
[

We have

Recall that for a connected reductive group
A
, we have
H_{A}^{*}=S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}
. We regard
\iota_{a}:H_{A}^{*}\to\mathbb{C}
as the algebra homomorphism given by evaluating a polynomial function on
\mathfrak{a}
at the semisimple element
a
. We denote
\mathbb{C}_{a}
as a
H_{A}^{*}
-algebra via this algebra homomorphism
\iota_{a}
.

Let
A
be a connected normal subgroup of
A^{+}
with
\mathfrak{R}:=A^{+}/A=\langle 1,\tau\rangle
. There is a
\tau
-action on
H_{A}^{*}
. Taking a semisimple element
a\in\mathfrak{a}
satisfying
\mathfrak{R}\cdot a\subset A\cdot a
, we define a
\tau
-action on
\mathbb{C}_{a}
to be the trivial action. Since
\iota_{a}(\tau\cdot h)=h(\tau(a))=h(a)
for
h\in H_{A}^{*}=S(\mathfrak{a}^{*})^{A}
, the algebra homomorphism
\iota_{a}:H_{A}^{*}\to\mathbb{C}_{a}
is compatible with the
\tau
-action.

Therefore we can define a
\tau
-action on

\mathbb{C}_{a}\otimes_{H_{A}^{*}}\mathrm{Hom}_{D_{A}(X)}^{*}(\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{Res}^{A^{+}}_{A}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))

by
\tau(c\otimes f)=c\otimes\tau(f)
. Combining Proposition
4.5
, it is well defined.

We will check that the isomorphism (

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

Remark 4.12
.

Applying the equivariant decomposition theorem to the

-equivariant proper map

, we get

. Hence

Let

Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}^{*}(\widetilde{K}^{+},\widetilde{K}^{+})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}^{*}(\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}},\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0,\phi^{+},\psi^{+}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(L_{\phi^{+}},L_{\psi^{+}})\otimes\mathrm{Hom}^{k}(\mathrm{IC}_{\phi^{+}},\mathrm{IC}_{\psi^{+}})

Since

is connected, we have

. Taking a representative

Combining (

), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}},\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}})^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\phi^{+},\psi^{+}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(L_{\phi^{+}},L_{\psi^{+}})\otimes\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(\mathrm{IC}_{\phi^{+}},\mathrm{IC}_{\psi^{+}})^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\phi^{+},\psi^{+}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(L_{\phi^{+}},L_{\psi^{+}})\otimes\mathrm{Hom}^{0}_{D_{Z^{+}}(\mathfrak{g}^{a})}(\mathrm{IC}_{\phi^{+}},\mathrm{IC}_{\psi^{+}})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\phi^{+}}\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}L_{\phi^{+}}

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}^{*}(\widetilde{K}^{+},\widetilde{K}^{+})^{\tau}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}^{*}(\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}},\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}})^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0,\phi^{+},\psi^{+}}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(L_{\phi^{+}},L_{\psi^{+}})\otimes\mathrm{Hom}^{k}(\mathrm{IC}_{\phi^{+}},\mathrm{IC}_{\psi^{+}})^{\tau}

(4.40)

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Hom}^{0}(\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}},\widetilde{K}^{+^{\prime}})^{\tau}=\bigoplus_{\phi^{+}}\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{C}}L_{\phi^{+}}

where the kernel of this homomorphism is a two-sided nilpotent ideal. This nilpotent ideal is the radical of the finite dimensional algebra
\mathrm{Hom}^{*}(\widetilde{K}^{+},\widetilde{K}^{+})^{\tau}
, since

Denote
