and
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
is the restriction.

We summarize what has been proved in the above subsections and get the following theorem:

Theorem 6.13
.

For a tempered representation
\pi
which can be viewed as a unique subrepresentation of the unramified principal series:
\pi\hookrightarrow\sigma
, denote
\sigma^{\mathcal{J}}\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}V_{M}=\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(T,v)}\mathbb{C}_{(t,p^{1/2})}
and
\pi^{\mathcal{J}}\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}
. If we give an action
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
on
V_{M}=\mathcal{H}(M,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(T,v)}\mathbb{C}_{({t},p^{1/2})}
induced by the
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
action on
\mathcal{H}(M,v)
and the trivial action on
\mathbb{C}_{(t,p^{1/2})}
, we have an action
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
on
\bar{V}_{M}
by restriction.

The action on
\pi^{\mathcal{J}}=\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}
given by
\hat{\gamma}\cdot(g\otimes v)=\hat{\gamma}(g)\iota_{w_{M}^{-1}}^{0}\otimes\hat{\gamma}_{M}(v)
induces a Whittaker-normalized action from
\pi
to
\gamma^{*}\pi
.

The
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
action on
\bar{V}_{M}
is an isomorphism from
\bar{V}_{M}
to
\hat{\gamma}_{M}^{*}\bar{V}_{M}
. Thus we can view
\bar{V}_{M}
as an
\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)
module.

From Lusztig’s first reduction theorem for the twisted version (Proposition
6.11
), we get an
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)
module by

\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M^{+},v)}\bar{V}_{M}
.

Using the same method, we also have

\displaystyle\pi^{\mathcal{J}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G,v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M^{+},v)}\bar{V}_{M}

We have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M^{+},v)}\bar{V}_{M}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M^{+},v)}V_{M}

so the
\hat{\gamma}
action induced by
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(G^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(M^{+},v)}\bar{V}_{M}
is the same as the
\hat{\gamma}
action restricted from
\sigma
.
Then we have another description of the theorem

Theorem 6.14
.

For a tempered representation
\pi
which can be viewed as a unique subrepresentation of the unramified principal series:
\pi\hookrightarrow\sigma
, denote
\sigma^{\mathcal{J}}\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}V_{M}=\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(T,v)}\mathbb{C}_{(t,p^{1/2})}
and
\pi^{\mathcal{J}}\simeq\mathcal{H}(G,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(M,v)}\bar{V}_{M}
. If we give an action
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
on
V_{M}=\mathcal{H}(M,v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(T,v)}\mathbb{C}_{({t},p^{1/2})}
induced by the
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
action on
\mathcal{H}(M,v)
and the trivial action on
\mathbb{C}_{(t,p^{1/2})}
, we have an action
\hat{\gamma}_{M}
on
\bar{V}_{M}
by restriction. Thus we can view
\bar{V}_{M}
as an
\mathcal{H}(M^{+},v)
module.

Then, by Lusztig’s first reduction theorem for the twisted version (Proposition
6.11
), we obtain a
\mathcal{G}^{+}
representation
\pi
with a Whittaker-normalized
\gamma
-action.

6.3.
Geometry and the main theorem

We use the notation of Section 5.1.

Recall
\mathcal{Q}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\cdots\times\mathcal{Q}^{0}\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{k}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Since
n_{k+1-i}=n_{i}
, for convenience, we write

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathcal{Q}^{0}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

and its complex dual group:

\displaystyle{{Q}}:=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times{Q^{0}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})

Define
\mathcal{Q}^{+}:=\mathcal{Q}\rtimes{\Gamma}
,

Q^{+}:=Q\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
.

We write the representation
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}:=\mathrm{St}(z_{1},n_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{St}(z_{\bar{k}},n_{\bar{k}})\otimes\pi^{0}\otimes\mathrm{St}(-z_{\bar{k}},n_{\bar{k}})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{St}(-z_{1},n_{1})
of
\mathcal{Q}
, where
\pi^{0}
is an irreducible tempered representation of
\mathcal{Q}^{0}
.

Using

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}}:\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{Q})^{(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}\backslash\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}})-\mathrm{Mod}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}\backslash\mathcal{Q}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}})\simeq\mathcal{H}(Q,p^{1/2})

and

\Theta_{Q}:\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{(\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}p^{1/2})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(Q,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

we have
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}=(\Lambda_{\mathcal{J_{Q}}})^{-1}\circ\Theta_{Q}\circ\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}
.

In fact, from
[
2
]
, the functor
\Theta_{Q}
is the composition of

\mathrm{Exp}:\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{(\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}p^{1/2})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

and

\mathrm{Ind}:\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(Q,v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

Hence
\Lambda_{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}(\pi_{\mathcal{Q}})=\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)}^{\mathcal{H}(Q,v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}
.

For convenience, We write

\displaystyle\bar{{Q}}:=Z_{Q}(t)=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\bar{Q}^{0}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})

where
\bar{Q}^{0}=\mathrm{GL}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{m_{2}}(\mathbb{C})

Since each Steinberg representation
\mathrm{St}(z_{i},n_{i})
of
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
corresponds to a one dimensional module
\chi_{i}
of
\mathcal{H}(\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}(\mathbb{C}),p^{1/2})
given by:

\displaystyle\chi_{i}(T_{s_{\alpha}})

\displaystyle=-1

\displaystyle\alpha\in\Pi

\displaystyle\chi_{i}(\theta_{y})

\displaystyle=y(s)

\displaystyle s=(p^{z_{i}}p^{{\frac{1-n_{i}}{2}}},p^{z_{i}}p^{{\frac{3-n_{i}}{2}}},\cdots,p^{z_{i}}p^{{\frac{n_{i}-1}{2}}})

we can write

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)}^{\mathcal{H}(Q,v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}({Q},v)}\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)}({\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1})

Recall also that the irreducible representation
M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1)
of
\mathcal{Q}
is given by

\displaystyle\Lambda^{+}_{\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}}:\mathrm{Rep}(\mathcal{Q}^{+})^{(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}},1_{\mathrm{triv}})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}\backslash\mathcal{Q}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}})-\mathrm{Mod}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}}\backslash\mathcal{Q}^{+}/\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{Q}})\simeq\mathcal{H}(Q^{+},p^{1/2})\simeq\mathcal{H}(Q,p^{1/2})\rtimes\Gamma

and

\Theta^{+}_{Q}:\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{(\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}p^{1/2})}\simeq\mathcal{H}(Q^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

which means

\displaystyle M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1)=(\Lambda_{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}^{+})^{-1}\circ\Theta^{+}_{Q}\circ\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}

Note that
\mathcal{H}(Q^{+},v)=\mathcal{H}(Q,v)\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
and

\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}))=\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}))\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
. Let
{w}_{0}\in W
be a permutation that swaps the first block of size
{m_{1}}
with the last block of size
{m_{2}}
and let
\bar{w}_{0}
be a representative of
w_{0}
in
Q
.

The action
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}
on the Weyl group
W
of
Z_{Q}(t_{c})
and on the torus
T
of
Z_{Q}(t_{c})
is given by

(6.34)

\displaystyle\tau(w)

\displaystyle={w}_{0}\hat{\gamma}(w){w}^{-1}_{0}\quad w\in W

\displaystyle\tau(s)

\displaystyle=\bar{w}_{0}\hat{\gamma}(s)\bar{w}^{-1}_{0}\quad s\in T

Hence we have the
\mathfrak{R}
action on
\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}))
induced by (
6.34
).

From
[
2
]
, the functor
\Theta_{Q}^{+}
is the composition of

\mathrm{Exp}:\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}))-\mathrm{Mod}_{(\mathrm{log}t_{h},\mathrm{log}p^{1/2})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

and

\mathrm{Ind}:\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{H}(Q^{+},v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}

We also have
\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)=\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
, where the
\mathfrak{R}
action is induced by (
6.34
).

In the connected case,
\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)
is a subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}({Q},v)
, so the
\operatorname{Ind}
functor is given by tensor product. However,
\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)=\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}),v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
is not a subalgebra of
\mathcal{H}(Q^{+},v)=\mathcal{H}(Q,v)\rtimes\hat{\Gamma}
. Fortunately, we still have an algebra embedding when we consider their completion (Proposition
6.11
):

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)\simeq{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)}\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Q^{+},v)

where

\displaystyle\tau\mapsto e_{\varpi}(\iota_{{w_{0}}}^{0},\hat{\gamma})

and
\iota_{{w}_{0}}^{0}
is the intertwining element of
{w}_{0}
.

Thus for every
(\pi,V)\in\mathcal{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)-\mathrm{Mod}_{(t,p^{1/2})}
, we can write the induction as:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}(\pi)=\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Q^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)}V

where
{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)}
acts on
V
via
{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}),v)
.

Consequently, we can write

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}^{+}(M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1))

\displaystyle=M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1)^{\mathcal{J_{Q}}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+})

From Proposition
4.24
, we get
M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}\simeq M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{triv}}
with
J_{\tau}
. Now we consider the geometric intertwining
J_{\tau}
and translate it to the
p
-adic side.

Recall
t=\lambda_{\phi}(\mathrm{Fr})
,
\bar{Q}=Z_{Q}(t_{c})=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{m_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}
and
t^{\prime}=\phi(\mathrm{Fr})
.

Denote
\bar{t}=-\mathrm{log}|t^{\prime}|+d_{\nu}\begin{pmatrix}r_{0}&0\\
0&-r_{0}\end{pmatrix}=(\bar{t}_{1},\cdots,\bar{t}_{\bar{k}},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{2},{\bar{t}}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,{\bar{t}}_{1}^{\vee})
,

y=d_{\phi}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}=(y_{1},\cdots,y_{\bar{k}},y^{\prime}_{1},y^{\prime}_{2},y_{\bar{k}},\cdots,y_{1})
, where

y_{i}=d_{\nu_{n_{i}}}\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}
, each
y_{i}
is regular unipotent element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n_{i}}(\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{GL}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{C})
and
\mathrm{GL}_{m_{2}}(\mathbb{C})
.

Define

\displaystyle y^{*}=(y_{1},\cdots,y_{\bar{k}},0,0,y_{\bar{k}},\cdots,y_{1})

Lemma 6.15
.

The irreducible representation
M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}
is the unique submodule of
E_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}
.

Proof.

By (
4.17
), we have
m(M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+},E_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+})=\sum_{k}\mathrm{dim}H^{k}(i_{y^{*}}^{!}IC_{\phi^{+}}))^{1}
. Since the closure of the orbit of
y
is a nonsingular variety, we get
m(M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+},E_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+})=1
. Because the irreducible representation
M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}
is the unique submodule of
E_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},1}
, the lemma follows.
∎

Recall
\bar{t}=(\bar{t}_{1},\cdots,\bar{t}_{\bar{k}},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{2},{\bar{t}}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,{\bar{t}}_{1}^{\vee})
. Since
\bar{t}^{\prime}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime}_{2}
are diagonal matrices,
let us reorder their diagonal entries so that

\displaystyle\bar{t}’^{*}_{1}

\displaystyle=(\bar{t}’^{*}_{1}(1),\cdots,\bar{t}’^{*}_{1}({m_{1}}))

\displaystyle\bar{t}’^{*}_{2}

\displaystyle=(\bar{t}’^{*}_{2}(1),\cdots\bar{t}’^{*}_{2}({m_{2}}))

satisfy
\bar{t}’^{*}_{1}(i)\leq\bar{t}’^{*}_{1}(j)\quad(i\leq j)
and
\bar{t}’^{*}_{2}(i)\leq\bar{t}’^{*}_{2}(j)\quad(i\leq j)

Set

\displaystyle\bar{t}^{*}=(\bar{t}_{1},\cdots,\bar{t}_{\bar{k}},\bar{t}^{\prime*}_{1},\bar{t}^{\prime*}_{2},{\bar{t}}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,{\bar{t}}_{1}^{\vee})

Then
\tau(\bar{t}^{*})=\bar{t}^{*}
(
\tau\in\mathfrak{R}
) and there is an
\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c}))=\mathbb{H}(Z_{Q}(t_{c}))\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
-module isomorphism
E_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},\rho}^{+}\simeq E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},\rho}^{+}
.

Recall
\bar{{Q}}=Z_{Q}(t_{c})=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{m_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}
, then
Z_{Q^{+}}(t_{c})
is generated by
\bar{{Q}}=Z_{Q}(t_{c})
and
(\bar{w}_{0},\hat{\gamma})
.
Let

\displaystyle\bar{{K}}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times_{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\times_{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}

and
\bar{{K}}^{+}
be the group generated by
\bar{{K}}
and
(\bar{w}_{0},\hat{\gamma})
. We have
\mathbb{H}(\bar{{K}}^{+})=\mathbb{H}(\bar{{K}})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
.

From Theorem
4.23
, we have
E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},\rho}^{+}\simeq\mathbb{H}(\bar{Q})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{H}(\bar{K})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},\rho}^{\bar{K}^{+},+}
.

Note that
E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}}=\mathbb{C}_{a}\otimes_{H_{M_{y^{*}}^{0}}}H^{M_{y}^{0}}_{*}(\mathcal{P}_{y^{*}}^{\bar{K}},{\dot{\mathcal{L}}})\simeq H_{*}^{\{1\}}(\mathcal{P}_{y^{*}}^{\bar{K},a},{\dot{\mathcal{L}}})

where
{\dot{\mathcal{L}}}
is the constant sheaf, and
\mathcal{P}_{y^{*}}^{\bar{K},a}
is a point.

Hence

\displaystyle E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}}=\bar{\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\bar{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\bar{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\bar{\chi}^{\prime}_{2}\otimes\bar{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes\bar{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

From
[
1
, Lemma 3.14]
, the
\tau
action on

E_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}}
is given by

(6.35)

\displaystyle I_{\tau}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}},b_{1},b_{2},a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,a^{\vee}_{1})=(a^{\vee}_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},b_{1},b_{2},a_{\bar{k}},\cdots,a_{1})

and we have the
\mathcal{H}(\bar{K}^{+},v)=\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
-module:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}}={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

Proposition 6.16
.

We have
\bar{{Q}}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\bar{Q}^{0}\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}
,
\bar{{K}}=\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times_{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\times_{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{\bar{k}}}(\mathbb{C})\times\cdots\times\mathrm{GL}_{n_{1}}
.

We have a
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)
-module:
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

Denote
\bar{T}=\times_{m_{1}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})\times_{m_{2}}\mathrm{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{C})
. Then
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)
and
\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)
are stable under the action
\tau
.

Define an
I_{\tau}^{0}
action on
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)}({{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes{\chi^{\prime}_{2}})
by
I_{\tau}^{0}(h,a)=\tau(h)\otimes a
. The irreducible module
\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}
is the unique submodule of
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)}({{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes{\chi^{\prime}_{2}})
. Then we give an action
I^{0}_{\tau}
on
\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}
by restriction from
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)}({{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes{\chi^{\prime}_{2}})
.

We give an
I_{\tau}
action on
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}
by:

\displaystyle I_{\tau}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}},b_{1},b_{2},a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,a^{\vee}_{1})=(a^{\vee}_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},I^{0}_{\tau}(b_{1},b_{2}),a_{\bar{k}},\cdots,a_{1})

Then using this
I_{\tau}
action we have
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M^{+}_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}\simeq\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{triv}}
as
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
-modules.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E^{+}_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E^{+}_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}(\mathbb{H}(\bar{Q})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{H}(\bar{K})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},\rho}^{\bar{K}^{+},+})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},\rho}^{\bar{K}^{+},+})

Use the same argument, we also have

(6.36)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}})

(6.37)

\displaystyle\simeq{\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)}({{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes{\chi^{\prime}_{2}})\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

Since
\tau(\bar{t}^{*})=\bar{t}^{*}
, the completion of
\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
with the corresponding central character is
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
and the completion of
\mathbb{H}(\bar{K})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
with the corresponding central character is
\widehat{\mathbb{H}}(\bar{K})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
.

Using
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{H}}(\bar{K})\rtimes\mathfrak{R}
and (
6.35
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}^{\bar{K}^{+},+}\simeq\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}^{\bar{K}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{triv}}

where
I_{\tau}^{\bar{K}}
is given by

\displaystyle I_{\tau}^{\bar{K}}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}},b_{1},b_{2},a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,a^{\vee}_{1})=(a^{\vee}_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},b_{1},b_{2},a_{\bar{k}},\cdots,a_{1})

on
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}^{\bar{K}}={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

Using the isomorphism of
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)
-modules

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)\rtimes\mathfrak{R}}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}^{\bar{K}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{\mathrm{triv}})\simeq\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}})

we can get the
I_{\tau}
action on
\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{K},v)}(\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0}}^{\bar{K}})
given by
I_{\tau}(h\otimes v):=\tau(h)\otimes I_{\tau}^{\bar{K}}(v)
.

Using the expression (
6.37
), we get the
I_{\tau}
action on
{\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\mathcal{H}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}(\bar{T},v)}({{\chi}^{\prime}_{1}}\otimes{\chi^{\prime}_{2}})\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}
as:

\displaystyle I_{\tau}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}},h\otimes(b_{1},b_{2}),a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,a^{\vee}_{1})=(a^{\vee}_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\tau(h)\otimes(b_{1},b_{2}),a_{\bar{k}},\cdots,a_{1})

The irreducible module
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M^{+}_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}
is the unique submodule of
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E^{+}_{y^{*},\bar{t},r_{0},1}\simeq\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}E^{+}_{y^{*},\bar{t}^{*},r_{0},1}
. Hence if we give the
I_{\tau}
action on
\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2}\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}
by restricted from the former, we obtain the proposition.
∎

Theorem 6.17
.

There is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{G}^{+}
representations

(M(\phi,1))^{+}_{W}\simeq M^{+}(\phi,1)

Proof.

We have

(6.38)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}(Q,v)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}

(6.39)

\displaystyle={\chi}_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}(Q^{0},v)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)}(\pi_{1}\otimes\pi_{2})\otimes{\chi}_{\bar{k}}^{\vee}\otimes\cdots\otimes{\chi}^{\vee}_{1}

Using

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Q^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Q,v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathcal{H}}(Q,v)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}

we have

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}\circ(g\otimes v)

\displaystyle=\hat{\gamma}(g)\iota_{w^{-1}}^{0}\otimes I_{\tau}(v)

where the
I_{\tau}
action comes from Proposition
6.16
.
Since
\iota_{w^{-1}}^{0}\in_{\mathcal{F}}{\mathcal{H}}(\bar{Q}^{0},v)
, using the expression (
6.38
), we get the
I_{\hat{\gamma}}
action on
{\mathcal{H}}(Q,v)\otimes_{{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0}}
:

\displaystyle I_{\hat{\gamma}}(a_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}},h\otimes(b_{1},b_{2}),a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\cdots,a^{\vee}_{1})=(a^{\vee}_{1},\cdots,a_{\bar{k}}^{\vee},\hat{\gamma}(h)\iota_{w^{-1}}^{0}\otimes I_{\tau}^{0}(b_{1},b_{2}),a_{\bar{k}},\cdots,a_{1})

By Theorem
6.14
, the middle action
h\otimes(b_{1},b_{2})\mapsto\hat{\gamma}(h)\iota_{w^{-1}}^{0}\otimes I_{\tau}^{0}(b_{1},b_{2})
corresponds to a Whittaker normalized action
I_{W,\pi^{0}}
on
\pi^{0}
. Hence
I_{\hat{\gamma}}
corresponds to the action (
6.9
) on
\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}
. Finally we get

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}\simeq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Q^{+},v)\otimes_{e_{\varpi}\widehat{\mathcal{H}}(Z_{Q^{+}}(t),v)}\mathrm{Exp}\mathrm{IM}^{*}M_{y,\bar{t},r_{0},1}^{+}=M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1)

From Proposition
6.2
, we have
(E(\phi,1))^{+}_{W}\simeq i_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}\pi_{\mathcal{Q}}^{+}\simeq E^{+}(\phi,1)=i_{\mathcal{P}^{+}}M^{+}(\phi_{\mathcal{Q}},1)
. Using the definition, we conclude
(M(\phi,1))^{+}_{W}\simeq M^{+}(\phi,1)
.
∎
